I’m here because… this is hard to say out loud, honestly. After I eat too much, I keep going into the bathroom and making myself throw up because I’m scared I’ll gain weight. Yes… yeah, I do. Usually it’s at night, after the house is quiet and everyone’s kind of gone to bed or stopped moving around. I’ll go into the kitchen and it starts like I’m just going to have a little something, like maybe leftovers or a bowl of cereal, but then it turns into more and more.

Usually it’s at night, after the house is quiet and everyone’s kind of gone to bed or stopped moving around. And it’s not a normal amount. It’s like I keep going way past where a person would usually stop, and afterward it looks like half the pantry has been picked through. It feels embarrassing to say, but yes, it’s definitely more than most people would eat in that amount of time.\n\nDoctor: How often do these episodes of eating a large amount of food in a short period of time happen?\n\nPatient: It’s… it’s pretty often. For at least the past three months, I don’t think there’s really been a week where it didn’t happen at least once. And I wish I could say it was just once a week, but some weeks it’s more than that — several times, especially when I’m home more because of my back and I’m just kind of stuck there with the kitchen nearby.\n\nIt usually ends up being late at night, and then afterward I go and make myself throw up. It’s usually after those late-night eating episodes. For at least the last three months, there’s hardly been a week where I didn’t do it… eat like that and then go to the bathroom and make myself throw up afterward.

I’ve been trying to tell myself it’s not “that bad” if it’s not every day, but when I actually say it out loud, it’s been happening almost every week for months now, and some weeks a lot more than once.\n\nDoctor: How often do you make yourself throw up after eating?\n\nPatient: It’s usually after those late-night eating episodes. So… yeah, it’s been happening almost every week, and some weeks multiple times.\n\nDoctor: Do you do anything else to try to compensate for the amount you've eaten during these episodes, besides making yourself throw up?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t really know how to answer that exactly. The main thing I do is make myself throw up. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes… as far as purging goes, it’s the throwing up. That’s the thing I do after I feel like I’ve eaten too much. I’ll go into the bathroom and make myself do it because I get scared I’m going to gain weight, and in that moment it feels like I have to “undo” what I just did.

I know that probably sounds really messed up, and I’m embarrassed saying it out loud, but that’s why I’m here. It’s gotten to the point where it doesn’t feel like just a bad choice here and there. It feels like I keep ending up back in the bathroom doing the same thing, and I’m scared because I don’t feel like I can stop it on my own.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n8\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF50.2, F50.4\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I’m here because… this is hard to say out loud, honestly. After I eat too much, I keep going into the bathroom and making myself throw up because I’m scared I’ll gain weight. And it’s not like it happened once and I just moved on — it’s become this thing I keep doing, even when part of me is telling myself not to.\n\nI feel really ashamed talking about it, but it’s gotten to the point where I’m scared I can’t stop on my own. Like I’ll eat, panic about what I just ate, and then I feel like I have to undo it somehow. And I know that probably sounds awful, but that’s why I came in. I don’t feel in control of it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you often eat an amount of food that is larger than what most people would eat in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: Yes… yeah, I do. Usually it’s at night, after the house is quiet and everyone’s kind of gone to bed or stopped moving around. I’ll go into the kitchen and it starts like I’m just going to have a little something, like maybe leftovers or a bowl of cereal, but then it turns into more and more.\n\nI’ll eat whatever’s there — leftovers, chips, cereal, ice cream, sometimes just standing there in the kitchen instead of even making it like a real meal. And it’s not a normal amount. It’s like I keep going way past where a person would usually stop, and afterward it looks like half the pantry has been picked through. It feels embarrassing to say, but yes, it’s definitely more than most people would eat in that amount of time.\n\nDoctor: How often do these episodes of eating a large amount of food in a short period of time happen?\n\nPatient: It’s… it’s pretty often. For at least the past three months, I don’t think there’s really been a week where it didn’t happen at least once. And I wish I could say it was just once a week, but some weeks it’s more than that — several times, especially when I’m home more because of my back and I’m just kind of stuck there with the kitchen nearby.\n\nIt usually ends up being late at night, and then afterward I go and make myself throw up. So it’s not just the eating part — it’s like the whole pattern happens together. I’ve been trying to tell myself it’s not “that bad” if it’s not every day, but when I actually say it out loud, it’s been happening almost every week for months now, and some weeks a lot more than once.\n\nDoctor: How often do you make yourself throw up after eating?\n\nPatient: It’s usually after those late-night eating episodes. For at least the last three months, there’s hardly been a week where I didn’t do it… eat like that and then go to the bathroom and make myself throw up afterward.\n\nSome weeks it’s several times, not just once. It gets worse when I’m home more because of my back, because I’m around the kitchen and I don’t have as much to distract me. Then once I’ve eaten too much, I start panicking and it feels like I have to get rid of it. So… yeah, it’s been happening almost every week, and some weeks multiple times.\n\nDoctor: Do you do anything else to try to compensate for the amount you've eaten during these episodes, besides making yourself throw up?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t really know how to answer that exactly. The main thing I do is make myself throw up. That’s the part that feels like the “fix” in my head after I’ve eaten too much, even though I know it sounds awful saying it that way.\n\nBesides that, I mean… I’ll feel guilty and upset and tell myself I need to be more careful the next day, but I’m not sure if that counts as compensating. I’m not doing some whole planned routine or anything that I can think of. It’s mostly the throwing up afterward. That’s the thing I keep going back to.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever exercise excessively to try to burn off calories after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I wouldn’t say I exercise to make up for it, no. That’s the thing I do after I feel like I’ve eaten too much. I’ll go into the bathroom and make myself do it because I get scared I’m going to gain weight, and in that moment it feels like I have to “undo” what I just did.\n\nI know that probably sounds really messed up, and I’m embarrassed saying it out loud, but that’s why I’m here. I’m not really sure, honestly. I haven’t been tracking it closely, like I’m not weighing myself in some organized way or keeping a record, so I don’t want to give you a number and have it be wrong.

After I eat too much, I keep going into the bathroom and making myself throw up because I’m scared I’ll gain weight. Like I’ll eat, panic about what I just ate, and then I feel like I have to undo it somehow. And I know that probably sounds awful, but that’s why I came in. I don’t feel in control of it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you often eat an amount of food that is larger than what most people would eat in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: Yes… yeah, I do. Usually it’s at night, after the house is quiet and everyone’s kind of gone to bed or stopped moving around. I’ll go into the kitchen and it starts like I’m just going to have a little something, like maybe leftovers or a bowl of cereal, but then it turns into more and more.\n\nI’ll eat whatever’s there — leftovers, chips, cereal, ice cream, sometimes just standing there in the kitchen instead of even making it like a real meal. And it’s not a normal amount. It’s like I keep going way past where a person would usually stop, and afterward it looks like half the pantry has been picked through. It feels embarrassing to say, but yes, it’s definitely more than most people would eat in that amount of time.\n\nDoctor: How often do these episodes of eating a large amount of food in a short period of time happen?\n\nPatient: It’s… it’s pretty often. For at least the past three months, I don’t think there’s really been a week where it didn’t happen at least once. And I wish I could say it was just once a week, but some weeks it’s more than that — several times, especially when I’m home more because of my back and I’m just kind of stuck there with the kitchen nearby.\n\nIt usually ends up being late at night, and then afterward I go and make myself throw up. For at least the last three months, there’s hardly been a week where I didn’t do it… eat like that and then go to the bathroom and make myself throw up afterward.\n\nSome weeks it’s several times, not just once. Usually it’s at night, after the house is quiet and everyone’s kind of gone to bed or stopped moving around. For at least the past three months, I don’t think there’s really been a week where it didn’t happen at least once. So it’s not just the eating part — it’s like the whole pattern happens together. I’ve been trying to tell myself it’s not “that bad” if it’s not every day, but when I actually say it out loud, it’s been happening almost every week for months now, and some weeks a lot more than once.\n\nDoctor: How often do you make yourself throw up after eating?\n\nPatient: It’s usually after those late-night eating episodes. For at least the last three months, there’s hardly been a week where I didn’t do it… eat like that and then go to the bathroom and make myself throw up afterward.\n\nSome weeks it’s several times, not just once. Yes… yeah, it’s at least once a week. Honestly, for the last three months or so, I can’t really think of many weeks where it didn’t happen.

It’s usually late at night, when I’m already tired and upset or just kind of stuck in my head. I’ll eat a lot in a short amount of time, more than I meant to, and then I panic about it and end up going to the bathroom to make myself throw up afterward. Some weeks it’s not just once, either. It can be several times, especially if I’m home more because of my back and I’m not as busy or distracted.

I hate admitting that because it sounds so out of control, but… yes, it’s definitely happening at least weekly.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Not every single time, but… most of the time, yes. If I eat what feels like too much, especially when it happens fast like that, I almost immediately start thinking I’ve messed everything up and I’m going to gain weight, and then I feel like I have to go to the bathroom and make myself throw up.

That’s honestly a big part of why I’m here. It’s gotten to the point where after I overeat, I keep doing it, and I’m scared because it doesn’t feel like something I can just decide to stop anymore. Sometimes there might be an episode where I don’t do it, but then I’m so anxious afterward that I’m thinking about it the whole time. Most of the time I end up purging because I’m trying to undo it, I guess.

[

    "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n12\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF50.2, F50.4\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I’m here because… this is hard to say out loud, honestly. After I eat too much, I keep going into the bathroom and making myself throw up because I’m scared I’ll gain weight. And it’s not like it happened once and I just moved on — it’s become this thing I keep doing, even when part of me is telling myself not to.\n\nI feel really ashamed talking about it, but it’s gotten to the point where I’m scared I can’t stop on my own. Like I’ll eat, panic about what I just ate, and then I feel like I have to undo it somehow. And I know that probably sounds awful, but that’s why I came in. I don’t feel in control of it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you often eat an amount of food that is larger than what most people would eat in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: Yes… yeah, I do. Usually it’s at night, after the house is quiet and everyone’s kind of gone to bed or stopped moving around. I’ll go into the kitchen and it starts like I’m just going to have a little something, like maybe leftovers or a bowl of cereal, but then it turns into more and more.\n\nI’ll eat whatever’s there — leftovers, chips, cereal, ice cream, sometimes just standing there in the kitchen instead of even making it like a real meal. And it’s not a normal amount. It’s like I keep going way past where a person would usually stop, and afterward it looks like half the pantry has been picked through. It feels embarrassing to say, but yes, it’s definitely more than most people would eat in that amount of time.\n\nDoctor: How often do these episodes of eating a large amount of food in a short period of time happen?\n\nPatient: It’s… it’s pretty often. For at least the past three months, I don’t think there’s really been a week where it didn’t happen at least once. And I wish I could say it was just once a week, but some weeks it’s more than that — several times, especially when I’m home more because of my back and I’m just kind of stuck there with the kitchen nearby.\n\nIt usually ends up being late at night, and then afterward I go and make myself throw up. So it’s not just the eating part — it’s like the whole pattern happens together. I’ve been trying to tell myself it’s not “that bad” if it’s not every day, but when I actually say it out loud, it’s been happening almost every week for months now, and some weeks a lot more than once.\n\nDoctor: How often do you make yourself throw up after eating?\n\nPatient: It’s usually after those late-night eating episodes. For at least the last three months, there’s hardly been a week where I didn’t do it… eat like that and then go to the bathroom and make myself throw up afterward.\n\nSome weeks it’s several times, not just once. It gets worse when I’m home more because of my back, because I’m around the kitchen and I don’t have as much to distract me. Then once I’ve eaten too much, I start panicking and it feels like I have to get rid of it. So… yeah, it’s been happening almost every week, and some weeks multiple times.\n\nDoctor: Do you do anything else to try to compensate for the amount you've eaten during these episodes, besides making yourself throw up?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t really know how to answer that exactly. The main thing I do is make myself throw up. That’s the part that feels like the “fix” in my head after I’ve eaten too much, even though I know it sounds awful saying it that way.\n\nBesides that, I mean… I’ll feel guilty and upset and tell myself I need to be more careful the next day, but I’m not sure if that counts as compensating. I’m not doing some whole planned routine or anything that I can think of. It’s mostly the throwing up afterward. That’s the thing I keep going back to.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever exercise excessively to try to burn off calories after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I wouldn’t say I exercise to make up for it, no. Especially with my back, exercise isn’t something I’m doing a lot of anyway, and I don’t have some routine where I’m like, “Okay, I ate this, now I have to burn it off.”\n\nI might think about needing to move more or be “better” the next day, but I don’t usually actually go work out after it happens. The thing I end up doing is more… what I already said, going to the bathroom afterward. That’s the part I get stuck on.\n\nDoctor: Do you use laxatives or diuretics to try to compensate for the amount you've eaten during these episodes?\n\nPatient: No… I mean, not really. I don’t use laxatives or water pills or anything like that to make up for it.\n\nFor me it’s been more that after I eat too much, I panic about gaining weight, and I go to the bathroom and make myself throw up. That’s the thing I keep doing. It’s like in my head I tell myself that’s the way to undo it, even though I know it’s not really okay, and honestly it scares me because it’s gotten to where I don’t feel like I can just stop.\n\nDoctor: Is making yourself throw up the only way you try to purge after eating too much?\n\nPatient: Yes… as far as purging goes, it’s the throwing up. That’s the thing I do after I feel like I’ve eaten too much. I’ll go into the bathroom and make myself do it because I get scared I’m going to gain weight, and in that moment it feels like I have to “undo” what I just did.\n\nI know that probably sounds really messed up, and I’m embarrassed saying it out loud, but that’s why I’m here. It’s gotten to the point where it doesn’t feel like just a bad choice here and there. It feels like I keep ending up back in the bathroom doing the same thing, and I’m scared because I don’t feel like I can stop it on my own.\n\nDoctor: Have you had significant changes in your weight over the past few months?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure, honestly. I haven’t been tracking it closely, like I’m not weighing myself in some organized way or keeping a record, so I don’t want to give you a number and have it be wrong.\n\nI feel like my weight kind of goes up and down, or at least it feels that way to me depending on the day, but I don’t know if it’s been a significant change over the last few months. Sometimes my clothes feel different and then other times they don’t, so I get in my head about it and it’s hard for me to tell what’s real versus what I’m just worrying about.\n\nSo… I guess the honest answer is I don’t know. I haven’t noticed a big clear change that I could point to, but I also haven’t been measuring it carefully.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any periods of time where you lost a significant amount of weight, only to regain it later?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I can say that for sure. I mean, I’ve had times where I felt like I looked smaller or my clothes fit a little looser, and then later I’d feel like everything was tight again, but I don’t know if that was a significant amount of weight or just me being really focused on it.\n\nI haven’t really tracked it in a way where I could say, like, “I lost this much and gained it back.” It’s more that my perception of my body changes a lot, and I get really anxious about it, so it’s hard for me to tell what’s actually happening versus what I’m afraid is happening. For at least the last three months, there’s hardly been a week where I didn’t do it… eat like that and then go to the bathroom and make myself throw up afterward.\n\nSome weeks it’s several times, not just once. It gets worse when I’m home more because of my back, because I’m around the kitchen and I don’t have as much to distract me. Then once I’ve eaten too much, I start panicking and it feels like I have to get rid of it. So… yeah, it’s been happening almost every week, and some weeks multiple times.\n\nDoctor: Do you do anything else to try to compensate for the amount you've eaten during these episodes, besides making yourself throw up?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t really know how to answer that exactly. The main thing I do is make myself throw up. That’s the part that feels like the “fix” in my head after I’ve eaten too much, even though I know it sounds awful saying it that way.\n\nBesides that, I mean… I’ll feel guilty and upset and tell myself I need to be more careful the next day, but I’m not sure if that counts as compensating. I’m not doing some whole planned routine or anything that I can think of. It’s mostly the throwing up afterward. That’s the thing I keep going back to.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever exercise excessively to try to burn off calories after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I wouldn’t say I exercise to make up for it, no. Especially with my back, exercise isn’t something I’m doing a lot of anyway, and I don’t have some routine where I’m like, “Okay, I ate this, now I have to burn it off.”\n\nI might think about needing to move more or be “better” the next day, but I don’t usually actually go work out after it happens. The thing I end up doing is more… what I already said, going to the bathroom afterward. That’s the part I get stuck on.\n\nDoctor: Do you use laxatives or diuretics to try to compensate for the amount you've eaten during these episodes?\n\nPatient: No… I mean, not really. I don’t use laxatives or water pills or anything like that to make up for it.\n\nFor me it’s been more that after I eat too much, I panic about gaining weight, and I go to the bathroom and make myself throw up. That’s the thing I keep doing. It’s like in my head I tell myself that’s the way to undo it, even though I know it’s not really okay, and honestly it scares me because it’s gotten to where I don’t feel like I can just stop.\n\nDoctor: Is making yourself throw up the only way you try to purge after eating too much?\n\nPatient: Yes… as far as purging goes, it’s the throwing up. That’s the thing I do after I feel like I’ve eaten too much. I’ll go into the bathroom and make myself do it because I get scared I’m going to gain weight, and in that moment it feels like I have to “undo” what I just did.\n\nI know that probably sounds really messed up, and I’m embarrassed saying it out loud, but that’s why I’m here. It’s gotten to the point where it doesn’t feel like just a bad choice here and there. It feels like I keep ending up back in the bathroom doing the same thing, and I’m scared because I don’t feel like I can stop it on my own.\n\nDoctor: Have you had significant changes in your weight over the past few months?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure, honestly. I haven’t been tracking it closely, like I’m not weighing myself in some organized way or keeping a record, so I don’t want to give you a number and have it be wrong.\n\nI feel like my weight kind of goes up and down, or at least it feels that way to me depending on the day, but I don’t know if it’s been a significant change over the last few months. Sometimes my clothes feel different and then other times they don’t, so I get in my head about it and it’s hard for me to tell what’s real versus what I’m just worrying about.\n\nSo… I guess the honest answer is I don’t know. I haven’t noticed a big clear change that I could point to, but I also haven’t been measuring it carefully.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any periods of time where you lost a significant amount of weight, only to regain it later?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I can say that for sure. I mean, I’ve had times where I felt like I looked smaller or my clothes fit a little looser, and then later I’d feel like everything was tight again, but I don’t know if that was a significant amount of weight or just me being really focused on it.\n\nI haven’t really tracked it in a way where I could say, like, “I lost this much and gained it back.” It’s more that my perception of my body changes a lot, and I get really anxious about it, so it’s hard for me to tell what’s actually happening versus what I’m afraid is happening. So… no clear period that I can point to, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you have these episodes of eating a large amount of food in a short period of time at least once a week?\n\nPatient: Yes… yeah, it’s at least once a week. Honestly, for the last three months or so, I can’t really think of many weeks where it didn’t happen.\n\nIt’s usually late at night, when I’m already tired and upset or just kind of stuck in my head. I’ll eat a lot in a short amount of time, more than I meant to, and then I panic about it and end up going to the bathroom to make myself throw up afterward. Some weeks it’s not just once, either. It can be several times, especially if I’m home more because of my back and I’m not as busy or distracted.\n\nI hate admitting that because it sounds so out of control, but… yes, it’s definitely happening at least weekly.\n\nDoctor: Does making yourself throw up happen after every episode of eating a large amount of food in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: Not every single time, but… most of the time, yes. If I eat what feels like too much, especially when it happens fast like that, I almost immediately start thinking I’ve messed everything up and I’m going to gain weight, and then I feel like I have to go to the bathroom and make myself throw up.\n\nThat’s honestly a big part of why I’m here. It’s gotten to the point where after I overeat, I keep doing it, and I’m scared because it doesn’t feel like something I can just decide to stop anymore. Sometimes there might be an episode where I don’t do it, but then I’m so anxious afterward that I’m thinking about it the whole time. Most of the time I end up purging because I’m trying to undo it, I guess.\n\nDoctor: When you make yourself throw up after eating, is it because you're trying to get rid of the calories you just consumed?\n\nPatient: Yes… that’s what it is. I wish I could say it was just because I felt sick or uncomfortable, but honestly, when I make myself throw up, it’s because I’m scared of what I just ate staying in me. I start thinking about the calories and gaining weight, and it feels like I have to get it out right away.\n\nThat’s really why I’m here. After I eat too much, I keep going to the bathroom and making myself throw up so I won’t gain weight, and it’s gotten to where it scares me because I don’t feel like I can stop once that panic starts.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Yes… mostly, yes. That’s the honest answer. It’s not just that I feel physically full or uncomfortable, although that can be part of what sets off the panic. If I eat what feels like too much, especially when it happens fast like that, I almost immediately start thinking I’ve messed everything up and I’m going to gain weight, and then I feel like I have to go to the bathroom and make myself throw up.\n\nThat’s honestly a big part of why I’m here. It’s gotten to the point where after I overeat, I keep doing it, and I’m scared because it doesn’t feel like something I can just decide to stop anymore. Sometimes there might be an episode where I don’t do it, but then I’m so anxious afterward that I’m thinking about it the whole time. Most of the time I end up purging because I’m trying to undo it, I guess.\n\nDoctor: When you make yourself throw up after eating, is it because you're trying to get rid of the calories you just consumed?\n\nPatient: Yes… that’s what it is. I wish I could say it was just because I felt sick or uncomfortable, but honestly, when I make myself throw up, it’s because I’m scared of what I just ate staying in me. I start thinking about the calories and gaining weight, and it feels like I have to get it out right away.\n\nThat’s really why I’m here. After I eat too much, I keep going to the bathroom and making myself throw up so I won’t gain weight, and it’s gotten to where it scares me because I don’t feel like I can stop once that panic starts.\n\nDoctor: Is making yourself throw up after eating mostly about trying to control your weight?\n\nPatient: Yes… mostly, yes. That’s the honest answer. It’s not just that I feel physically full or uncomfortable, although that can be part of what sets off the panic. The main thing in my head is, “I ate too much, I’m going to gain weight, I have to undo this.” And then I end up in the bathroom making myself throw up.\n\nI’m here because it’s gotten to where after I eat too much, I keep doing that — I make myself throw up so I won’t gain weight — and it scares me because once that thought starts, it feels like I can’t stop myself. Sometimes, yes… but it’s not common. I mean, there are times when I eat a lot quickly and I don’t throw up afterward, but even then it’s like my head doesn’t let it go. I’ll sit there feeling panicked and guilty, thinking about what I ate and whether it’s going to make me gain weight.

I don’t feel in control of it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you often eat an amount of food that is larger than what most people would eat in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: Yes… yeah, I do. Usually it’s at night, after the house is quiet and everyone’s kind of gone to bed or stopped moving around. I’ll go into the kitchen and it starts like I’m just going to have a little something, like maybe leftovers or a bowl of cereal, but then it turns into more and more.\n\nI’ll eat whatever’s there — leftovers, chips, cereal, ice cream, sometimes just standing there in the kitchen instead of even making it like a real meal. And it’s not a normal amount. It’s like I keep going way past where a person would usually stop, and afterward it looks like half the pantry has been picked through. It feels embarrassing to say, but yes, it’s definitely more than most people would eat in that amount of time.\n\nDoctor: How often do these episodes of eating a large amount of food in a short period of time happen?\n\nPatient: It’s… it’s pretty often. For at least the past three months, I don’t think there’s really been a week where it didn’t happen at least once. And I wish I could say it was just once a week, but some weeks it’s more than that — several times, especially when I’m home more because of my back and I’m just kind of stuck there with the kitchen nearby.\n\nIt usually ends up being late at night, and then afterward I go and make myself throw up. So it’s not just the eating part — it’s like the whole pattern happens together. I’ve been trying to tell myself it’s not “that bad” if it’s not every day, but when I actually say it out loud, it’s been happening almost every week for months now, and some weeks a lot more than once.\n\nDoctor: How often do you make yourself throw up after eating?\n\nPatient: It’s usually after those late-night eating episodes. For at least the last three months, there’s hardly been a week where I didn’t do it… eat like that and then go to the bathroom and make myself throw up afterward.\n\nSome weeks it’s several times, not just once. It gets worse when I’m home more because of my back, because I’m around the kitchen and I don’t have as much to distract me. Then once I’ve eaten too much, I start panicking and it feels like I have to get rid of it. So… yeah, it’s been happening almost every week, and some weeks multiple times.\n\nDoctor: Do you do anything else to try to compensate for the amount you've eaten during these episodes, besides making yourself throw up?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t really know how to answer that exactly. The main thing I do is make myself throw up. That’s the part that feels like the “fix” in my head after I’ve eaten too much, even though I know it sounds awful saying it that way.\n\nBesides that, I mean… I’ll feel guilty and upset and tell myself I need to be more careful the next day, but I’m not sure if that counts as compensating. I’m not doing some whole planned routine or anything that I can think of. It’s mostly the throwing up afterward. That’s the thing I keep going back to.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever exercise excessively to try to burn off calories after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I wouldn’t say I exercise to make up for it, no. Especially with my back, exercise isn’t something I’m doing a lot of anyway, and I don’t have some routine where I’m like, “Okay, I ate this, now I have to burn it off.”\n\nI might think about needing to move more or be “better” the next day, but I don’t usually actually go work out after it happens. The thing I end up doing is more… what I already said, going to the bathroom afterward. That’s the part I get stuck on.\n\nDoctor: Do you use laxatives or diuretics to try to compensate for the amount you've eaten during these episodes?\n\nPatient: No… I mean, not really. I don’t use laxatives or water pills or anything like that to make up for it.\n\nFor me it’s been more that after I eat too much, I panic about gaining weight, and I go to the bathroom and make myself throw up. That’s the thing I keep doing. It’s like in my head I tell myself that’s the way to undo it, even though I know it’s not really okay, and honestly it scares me because it’s gotten to where I don’t feel like I can just stop.\n\nDoctor: Is making yourself throw up the only way you try to purge after eating too much?\n\nPatient: Yes… as far as purging goes, it’s the throwing up. That’s the thing I do after I feel like I’ve eaten too much. I’ll go into the bathroom and make myself do it because I get scared I’m going to gain weight, and in that moment it feels like I have to “undo” what I just did.\n\nI know that probably sounds really messed up, and I’m embarrassed saying it out loud, but that’s why I’m here. It’s gotten to the point where it doesn’t feel like just a bad choice here and there. It feels like I keep ending up back in the bathroom doing the same thing, and I’m scared because I don’t feel like I can stop it on my own.\n\nDoctor: Have you had significant changes in your weight over the past few months?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure, honestly. I haven’t been tracking it closely, like I’m not weighing myself in some organized way or keeping a record, so I don’t want to give you a number and have it be wrong.\n\nI feel like my weight kind of goes up and down, or at least it feels that way to me depending on the day, but I don’t know if it’s been a significant change over the last few months. Sometimes my clothes feel different and then other times they don’t, so I get in my head about it and it’s hard for me to tell what’s real versus what I’m just worrying about.\n\nSo… I guess the honest answer is I don’t know. I haven’t noticed a big clear change that I could point to, but I also haven’t been measuring it carefully.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any periods of time where you lost a significant amount of weight, only to regain it later?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I can say that for sure. I mean, I’ve had times where I felt like I looked smaller or my clothes fit a little looser, and then later I’d feel like everything was tight again, but I don’t know if that was a significant amount of weight or just me being really focused on it.\n\nI haven’t really tracked it in a way where I could say, like, “I lost this much and gained it back.” It’s more that my perception of my body changes a lot, and I get really anxious about it, so it’s hard for me to tell what’s actually happening versus what I’m afraid is happening. So… no clear period that I can point to, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you have these episodes of eating a large amount of food in a short period of time at least once a week?\n\nPatient: Yes… yeah, it’s at least once a week. Honestly, for the last three months or so, I can’t really think of many weeks where it didn’t happen.\n\nIt’s usually late at night, when I’m already tired and upset or just kind of stuck in my head. I’ll eat a lot in a short amount of time, more than I meant to, and then I panic about it and end up going to the bathroom to make myself throw up afterward. Some weeks it’s not just once, either. It can be several times, especially if I’m home more because of my back and I’m not as busy or distracted.\n\nI hate admitting that because it sounds so out of control, but… yes, it’s definitely happening at least weekly.\n\nDoctor: Does making yourself throw up happen after every episode of eating a large amount of food in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: Not every single time, but… most of the time, yes. If I eat what feels like too much, especially when it happens fast like that, I almost immediately start thinking I’ve messed everything up and I’m going to gain weight, and then I feel like I have to go to the bathroom and make myself throw up.\n\nThat’s honestly a big part of why I’m here. It’s gotten to the point where after I overeat, I keep doing it, and I’m scared because it doesn’t feel like something I can just decide to stop anymore. Sometimes there might be an episode where I don’t do it, but then I’m so anxious afterward that I’m thinking about it the whole time. Most of the time I end up purging because I’m trying to undo it, I guess.\n\nDoctor: When you make yourself throw up after eating, is it because you're trying to get rid of the calories you just consumed?\n\nPatient: Yes… that’s what it is. I wish I could say it was just because I felt sick or uncomfortable, but honestly, when I make myself throw up, it’s because I’m scared of what I just ate staying in me. I start thinking about the calories and gaining weight, and it feels like I have to get it out right away.\n\nThat’s really why I’m here. After I eat too much, I keep going to the bathroom and making myself throw up so I won’t gain weight, and it’s gotten to where it scares me because I don’t feel like I can stop once that panic starts.\n\nDoctor: Is making yourself throw up after eating mostly about trying to control your weight?\n\nPatient: Yes… mostly, yes. That’s the honest answer. It’s not just that I feel physically full or uncomfortable, although that can be part of what sets off the panic. The main thing in my head is, “I ate too much, I’m going to gain weight, I have to undo this.” And then I end up in the bathroom making myself throw up.\n\nI’m here because it’s gotten to where after I eat too much, I keep doing that — I make myself throw up so I won’t gain weight — and it scares me because once that thought starts, it feels like I can’t stop myself. I know it probably sounds repetitive, but that’s exactly what it feels like in the moment: like if I don’t get it out, I’ve lost control of my weight completely.\n\nDoctor: Are there times when you eat a large amount of food in a short period, but you don't make yourself throw up afterward?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, yes… but it’s not common. I mean, there are times when I eat a lot quickly and I don’t throw up afterward, but even then it’s like my head doesn’t let it go. I’ll sit there feeling panicked and guilty, thinking about what I ate and whether it’s going to make me gain weight.\n\nMost of the time, though, I do end up going to the bathroom and making myself throw up. That’s really why I’m here — because after I eat too much, I keep doing it so I won’t gain weight, and it’s gotten to the point where I’m scared I can’t stop myself. Even if I tell myself I’m not going to, once I feel like I’ve eaten too much, it starts feeling almost automatic.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

Even if I tell myself I’m not going to, once I feel like I’ve eaten too much, it starts feeling almost automatic.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you make yourself throw up after almost every episode of eating a large amount of food in a short period of time?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes… almost every time. If I eat what feels like too much, especially if it happens fast, I pretty much go straight into that panic in my head. I start thinking, “I can’t let this stay in me, I’m going to gain weight,” and then I end up in the bathroom making myself throw up.

I don’t want to make it sound like it’s some casual thing, because it doesn’t feel casual at all. It feels like once I’ve crossed that line with food, I don’t know how to just sit with it. I tell myself I should stop, or that I shouldn’t do it this time, but then the fear gets so loud that I feel like I have to. That’s really why I’m here — after I eat too much, I keep making myself throw up so I won’t gain weight, and it’s gotten to where I’m scared I can’t stop doing it.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n16\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF50.2, F50.4\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I’m here because… this is hard to say out loud, honestly. After I eat too much, I keep going into the bathroom and making myself throw up because I’m scared I’ll gain weight. And it’s not like it happened once and I just moved on — it’s become this thing I keep doing, even when part of me is telling myself not to.\n\nI feel really ashamed talking about it, but it’s gotten to the point where I’m scared I can’t stop on my own. Like I’ll eat, panic about what I just ate, and then I feel like I have to undo it somehow. And I know that probably sounds awful, but that’s why I came in. I don’t feel in control of it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you often eat an amount of food that is larger than what most people would eat in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: Yes… yeah, I do. Usually it’s at night, after the house is quiet and everyone’s kind of gone to bed or stopped moving around. I’ll go into the kitchen and it starts like I’m just going to have a little something, like maybe leftovers or a bowl of cereal, but then it turns into more and more.\n\nI’ll eat whatever’s there — leftovers, chips, cereal, ice cream, sometimes just standing there in the kitchen instead of even making it like a real meal. And it’s not a normal amount. It’s like I keep going way past where a person would usually stop, and afterward it looks like half the pantry has been picked through. It feels embarrassing to say, but yes, it’s definitely more than most people would eat in that amount of time.\n\nDoctor: How often do these episodes of eating a large amount of food in a short period of time happen?\n\nPatient: It’s… it’s pretty often. For at least the past three months, I don’t think there’s really been a week where it didn’t happen at least once. And I wish I could say it was just once a week, but some weeks it’s more than that — several times, especially when I’m home more because of my back and I’m just kind of stuck there with the kitchen nearby.\n\nIt usually ends up being late at night, and then afterward I go and make myself throw up. So it’s not just the eating part — it’s like the whole pattern happens together. I’ve been trying to tell myself it’s not “that bad” if it’s not every day, but when I actually say it out loud, it’s been happening almost every week for months now, and some weeks a lot more than once.\n\nDoctor: How often do you make yourself throw up after eating?\n\nPatient: It’s usually after those late-night eating episodes. For at least the last three months, there’s hardly been a week where I didn’t do it… eat like that and then go to the bathroom and make myself throw up afterward.\n\nSome weeks it’s several times, not just once. It gets worse when I’m home more because of my back, because I’m around the kitchen and I don’t have as much to distract me. Then once I’ve eaten too much, I start panicking and it feels like I have to get rid of it. So… yeah, it’s been happening almost every week, and some weeks multiple times.\n\nDoctor: Do you do anything else to try to compensate for the amount you've eaten during these episodes, besides making yourself throw up?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t really know how to answer that exactly. The main thing I do is make myself throw up. That’s the part that feels like the “fix” in my head after I’ve eaten too much, even though I know it sounds awful saying it that way.\n\nBesides that, I mean… I’ll feel guilty and upset and tell myself I need to be more careful the next day, but I’m not sure if that counts as compensating. I’m not doing some whole planned routine or anything that I can think of. It’s mostly the throwing up afterward. That’s the thing I keep going back to.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever exercise excessively to try to burn off calories after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I wouldn’t say I exercise to make up for it, no. Especially with my back, exercise isn’t something I’m doing a lot of anyway, and I don’t have some routine where I’m like, “Okay, I ate this, now I have to burn it off.”\n\nI might think about needing to move more or be “better” the next day, but I don’t usually actually go work out after it happens. The thing I end up doing is more… what I already said, going to the bathroom afterward. That’s the part I get stuck on.\n\nDoctor: Do you use laxatives or diuretics to try to compensate for the amount you've eaten during these episodes?\n\nPatient: No… I mean, not really. I don’t use laxatives or water pills or anything like that to make up for it.\n\nFor me it’s been more that after I eat too much, I panic about gaining weight, and I go to the bathroom and make myself throw up. That’s the thing I keep doing. It’s like in my head I tell myself that’s the way to undo it, even though I know it’s not really okay, and honestly it scares me because it’s gotten to where I don’t feel like I can just stop.\n\nDoctor: Is making yourself throw up the only way you try to purge after eating too much?\n\nPatient: Yes… as far as purging goes, it’s the throwing up. That’s the thing I do after I feel like I’ve eaten too much. I’ll go into the bathroom and make myself do it because I get scared I’m going to gain weight, and in that moment it feels like I have to “undo” what I just did.\n\nI know that probably sounds really messed up, and I’m embarrassed saying it out loud, but that’s why I’m here. It’s gotten to the point where it doesn’t feel like just a bad choice here and there. It feels like I keep ending up back in the bathroom doing the same thing, and I’m scared because I don’t feel like I can stop it on my own.\n\nDoctor: Have you had significant changes in your weight over the past few months?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure, honestly. I haven’t been tracking it closely, like I’m not weighing myself in some organized way or keeping a record, so I don’t want to give you a number and have it be wrong.\n\nI feel like my weight kind of goes up and down, or at least it feels that way to me depending on the day, but I don’t know if it’s been a significant change over the last few months. Sometimes my clothes feel different and then other times they don’t, so I get in my head about it and it’s hard for me to tell what’s real versus what I’m just worrying about.\n\nSo… I guess the honest answer is I don’t know. I haven’t noticed a big clear change that I could point to, but I also haven’t been measuring it carefully.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any periods of time where you lost a significant amount of weight, only to regain it later?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I can say that for sure. I mean, I’ve had times where I felt like I looked smaller or my clothes fit a little looser, and then later I’d feel like everything was tight again, but I don’t know if that was a significant amount of weight or just me being really focused on it.\n\nI haven’t really tracked it in a way where I could say, like, “I lost this much and gained it back.” It’s more that my perception of my body changes a lot, and I get really anxious about it, so it’s hard for me to tell what’s actually happening versus what I’m afraid is happening. So… no clear period that I can point to, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you have these episodes of eating a large amount of food in a short period of time at least once a week?\n\nPatient: Yes… yeah, it’s at least once a week. Honestly, for the last three months or so, I can’t really think of many weeks where it didn’t happen.\n\nIt’s usually late at night, when I’m already tired and upset or just kind of stuck in my head. I’ll eat a lot in a short amount of time, more than I meant to, and then I panic about it and end up going to the bathroom to make myself throw up afterward. Some weeks it’s not just once, either. It can be several times, especially if I’m home more because of my back and I’m not as busy or distracted.\n\nI hate admitting that because it sounds so out of control, but… yes, it’s definitely happening at least weekly.\n\nDoctor: Does making yourself throw up happen after every episode of eating a large amount of food in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: Not every single time, but… most of the time, yes. If I eat what feels like too much, especially when it happens fast like that, I almost immediately start thinking I’ve messed everything up and I’m going to gain weight, and then I feel like I have to go to the bathroom and make myself throw up.\n\nThat’s honestly a big part of why I’m here. It’s gotten to the point where after I overeat, I keep doing it, and I’m scared because it doesn’t feel like something I can just decide to stop anymore. Sometimes there might be an episode where I don’t do it, but then I’m so anxious afterward that I’m thinking about it the whole time. Most of the time I end up purging because I’m trying to undo it, I guess.\n\nDoctor: When you make yourself throw up after eating, is it because you're trying to get rid of the calories you just consumed?\n\nPatient: Yes… that’s what it is. I wish I could say it was just because I felt sick or uncomfortable, but honestly, when I make myself throw up, it’s because I’m scared of what I just ate staying in me. It can be several times, especially if I’m home more because of my back and I’m not as busy or distracted.\n\nI hate admitting that because it sounds so out of control, but… yes, it’s definitely happening at least weekly.\n\nDoctor: Does making yourself throw up happen after every episode of eating a large amount of food in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: Not every single time, but… most of the time, yes. It’s gotten to the point where after I overeat, I keep doing it, and I’m scared because it doesn’t feel like something I can just decide to stop anymore. I mean… I think most of the time it is, yes. That’s the part I’m embarrassed to say out loud, but it’s true. After I eat too much, it’s like my brain goes straight to, “You’re going to gain weight, you have to fix this.” And then I end up going to the bathroom and making myself throw up.

That’s the honest answer. It’s not just that I feel physically full or uncomfortable, although that can be part of what sets off the panic. The main thing in my head is, “I ate too much, I’m going to gain weight, I have to undo this.” And then I end up in the bathroom making myself throw up.\n\nI’m here because it’s gotten to where after I eat too much, I keep doing that — I make myself throw up so I won’t gain weight — and it scares me because once that thought starts, it feels like I can’t stop myself. I know it probably sounds repetitive, but that’s exactly what it feels like in the moment: like if I don’t get it out, I’ve lost control of my weight completely.\n\nDoctor: Are there times when you eat a large amount of food in a short period, but you don't make yourself throw up afterward?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, yes… but it’s not common. I mean, there are times when I eat a lot quickly and I don’t throw up afterward, but even then it’s like my head doesn’t let it go. I’ll sit there feeling panicked and guilty, thinking about what I ate and whether it’s going to make me gain weight.\n\nMost of the time, though, I do end up going to the bathroom and making myself throw up. That’s really why I’m here — because after I eat too much, I keep doing it so I won’t gain weight, and it’s gotten to the point where I’m scared I can’t stop myself. Even if I tell myself I’m not going to, once I feel like I’ve eaten too much, it starts feeling almost automatic.\n\nDoctor: Do you make yourself throw up after almost every episode of eating a large amount of food in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: Yes… almost every time. If I eat what feels like too much, especially if it happens fast, I pretty much go straight into that panic in my head. I start thinking, “I can’t let this stay in me, I’m going to gain weight,” and then I end up in the bathroom making myself throw up.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound like it’s some casual thing, because it doesn’t feel casual at all. It feels like once I’ve crossed that line with food, I don’t know how to just sit with it. I tell myself I should stop, or that I shouldn’t do it this time, but then the fear gets so loud that I feel like I have to. That’s really why I’m here — after I eat too much, I keep making myself throw up so I won’t gain weight, and it’s gotten to where I’m scared I can’t stop doing it.\n\nDoctor: Is making yourself throw up always about trying to control your weight?\n\nPatient: I mean… I think most of the time it is, yes. That’s the part I’m embarrassed to say out loud, but it’s true. After I eat too much, it’s like my brain goes straight to, “You’re going to gain weight, you have to fix this.” And then I end up going to the bathroom and making myself throw up.\n\nIt’s not like I sit there calmly deciding it. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes… I do, at least at first. It’s like the panic spikes so high after I eat too much that making myself throw up feels like the only way to get it to come down. I hate saying that, because I know it sounds terrible, but in that moment I’m thinking, “Okay, now maybe I won’t gain weight from this,” and there’s this immediate kind of relief.

But it doesn’t last very long. Afterward I usually feel ashamed and scared, because I’m standing there in the bathroom realizing I did it again, even after I told myself I wouldn’t. That’s really what scares me — it’s gotten to where after I eat too much, I keep making myself throw up so I won’t gain weight, and I don’t feel like I can stop once that fear takes over.

---------- turn 18 | doctor:inference | doctor.build_inference_prompt() | model=meta-llama/Llama-3.3-70B-Instruct ----------
========== INPUT [doctor] ==========
[
  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n18\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF50.2, F50.4\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I’m here because… this is hard to say out loud, honestly. After I eat too much, I keep going into the bathroom and making myself throw up because I’m scared I’ll gain weight. And it’s not like it happened once and I just moved on — it’s become this thing I keep doing, even when part of me is telling myself not to.\n\nI feel really ashamed talking about it, but it’s gotten to the point where I’m scared I can’t stop on my own. Like I’ll eat, panic about what I just ate, and then I feel like I have to undo it somehow. And I know that probably sounds awful, but that’s why I came in. I don’t feel in control of it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you often eat an amount of food that is larger than what most people would eat in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: Yes… yeah, I do. Usually it’s at night, after the house is quiet and everyone’s kind of gone to bed or stopped moving around. I’ll go into the kitchen and it starts like I’m just going to have a little something, like maybe leftovers or a bowl of cereal, but then it turns into more and more.\n\nI’ll eat whatever’s there — leftovers, chips, cereal, ice cream, sometimes just standing there in the kitchen instead of even making it like a real meal. And it’s not a normal amount. It’s like I keep going way past where a person would usually stop, and afterward it looks like half the pantry has been picked through. It feels embarrassing to say, but yes, it’s definitely more than most people would eat in that amount of time.\n\nDoctor: How often do these episodes of eating a large amount of food in a short period of time happen?\n\nPatient: It’s… it’s pretty often. For at least the past three months, I don’t think there’s really been a week where it didn’t happen at least once. And I wish I could say it was just once a week, but some weeks it’s more than that — several times, especially when I’m home more because of my back and I’m just kind of stuck there with the kitchen nearby.\n\nIt usually ends up being late at night, and then afterward I go and make myself throw up. So it’s not just the eating part — it’s like the whole pattern happens together. I’ve been trying to tell myself it’s not “that bad” if it’s not every day, but when I actually say it out loud, it’s been happening almost every week for months now, and some weeks a lot more than once.\n\nDoctor: How often do you make yourself throw up after eating?\n\nPatient: It’s usually after those late-night eating episodes. For at least the last three months, there’s hardly been a week where I didn’t do it… eat like that and then go to the bathroom and make myself throw up afterward.\n\nSome weeks it’s several times, not just once. It gets worse when I’m home more because of my back, because I’m around the kitchen and I don’t have as much to distract me. Then once I’ve eaten too much, I start panicking and it feels like I have to get rid of it. So… yeah, it’s been happening almost every week, and some weeks multiple times.\n\nDoctor: Do you do anything else to try to compensate for the amount you've eaten during these episodes, besides making yourself throw up?\n\nPatient: I don’t… I don’t really know how to answer that exactly. The main thing I do is make myself throw up. That’s the part that feels like the “fix” in my head after I’ve eaten too much, even though I know it sounds awful saying it that way.\n\nBesides that, I mean… I’ll feel guilty and upset and tell myself I need to be more careful the next day, but I’m not sure if that counts as compensating. I’m not doing some whole planned routine or anything that I can think of. It’s mostly the throwing up afterward. That’s the thing I keep going back to.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever exercise excessively to try to burn off calories after one of these eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I wouldn’t say I exercise to make up for it, no. Especially with my back, exercise isn’t something I’m doing a lot of anyway, and I don’t have some routine where I’m like, “Okay, I ate this, now I have to burn it off.”\n\nI might think about needing to move more or be “better” the next day, but I don’t usually actually go work out after it happens. The thing I end up doing is more… what I already said, going to the bathroom afterward. That’s the part I get stuck on.\n\nDoctor: Do you use laxatives or diuretics to try to compensate for the amount you've eaten during these episodes?\n\nPatient: No… I mean, not really. I don’t use laxatives or water pills or anything like that to make up for it.\n\nFor me it’s been more that after I eat too much, I panic about gaining weight, and I go to the bathroom and make myself throw up. That’s the thing I keep doing. It’s like in my head I tell myself that’s the way to undo it, even though I know it’s not really okay, and honestly it scares me because it’s gotten to where I don’t feel like I can just stop.\n\nDoctor: Is making yourself throw up the only way you try to purge after eating too much?\n\nPatient: Yes… as far as purging goes, it’s the throwing up. That’s the thing I do after I feel like I’ve eaten too much. I’ll go into the bathroom and make myself do it because I get scared I’m going to gain weight, and in that moment it feels like I have to “undo” what I just did.\n\nI know that probably sounds really messed up, and I’m embarrassed saying it out loud, but that’s why I’m here. It’s gotten to the point where it doesn’t feel like just a bad choice here and there. It feels like I keep ending up back in the bathroom doing the same thing, and I’m scared because I don’t feel like I can stop it on my own.\n\nDoctor: Have you had significant changes in your weight over the past few months?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure, honestly. I haven’t been tracking it closely, like I’m not weighing myself in some organized way or keeping a record, so I don’t want to give you a number and have it be wrong.\n\nI feel like my weight kind of goes up and down, or at least it feels that way to me depending on the day, but I don’t know if it’s been a significant change over the last few months. Sometimes my clothes feel different and then other times they don’t, so I get in my head about it and it’s hard for me to tell what’s real versus what I’m just worrying about.\n\nSo… I guess the honest answer is I don’t know. I haven’t noticed a big clear change that I could point to, but I also haven’t been measuring it carefully.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any periods of time where you lost a significant amount of weight, only to regain it later?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I can say that for sure. I mean, I’ve had times where I felt like I looked smaller or my clothes fit a little looser, and then later I’d feel like everything was tight again, but I don’t know if that was a significant amount of weight or just me being really focused on it.\n\nI haven’t really tracked it in a way where I could say, like, “I lost this much and gained it back.” It’s more that my perception of my body changes a lot, and I get really anxious about it, so it’s hard for me to tell what’s actually happening versus what I’m afraid is happening. So… no clear period that I can point to, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you have these episodes of eating a large amount of food in a short period of time at least once a week?\n\nPatient: Yes… yeah, it’s at least once a week. Honestly, for the last three months or so, I can’t really think of many weeks where it didn’t happen.\n\nIt’s usually late at night, when I’m already tired and upset or just kind of stuck in my head. I’ll eat a lot in a short amount of time, more than I meant to, and then I panic about it and end up going to the bathroom to make myself throw up afterward. Some weeks it’s not just once, either. It can be several times, especially if I’m home more because of my back and I’m not as busy or distracted.\n\nI hate admitting that because it sounds so out of control, but… yes, it’s definitely happening at least weekly.\n\nDoctor: Does making yourself throw up happen after every episode of eating a large amount of food in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: Not every single time, but… most of the time, yes. If I eat what feels like too much, especially when it happens fast like that, I almost immediately start thinking I’ve messed everything up and I’m going to gain weight, and then I feel like I have to go to the bathroom and make myself throw up.\n\nThat’s honestly a big part of why I’m here. It’s gotten to the point where after I overeat, I keep doing it, and I’m scared because it doesn’t feel like something I can just decide to stop anymore. Sometimes there might be an episode where I don’t do it, but then I’m so anxious afterward that I’m thinking about it the whole time. Most of the time I end up purging because I’m trying to undo it, I guess.\n\nDoctor: When you make yourself throw up after eating, is it because you're trying to get rid of the calories you just consumed?\n\nPatient: Yes… that’s what it is. I wish I could say it was just because I felt sick or uncomfortable, but honestly, when I make myself throw up, it’s because I’m scared of what I just ate staying in me. I start thinking about the calories and gaining weight, and it feels like I have to get it out right away.\n\nThat’s really why I’m here. After I eat too much, I keep going to the bathroom and making myself throw up so I won’t gain weight, and it’s gotten to where it scares me because I don’t feel like I can stop once that panic starts.\n\nDoctor: Is making yourself throw up after eating mostly about trying to control your weight?\n\nPatient: Yes… mostly, yes. That’s the honest answer. It’s not just that I feel physically full or uncomfortable, although that can be part of what sets off the panic. The main thing in my head is, “I ate too much, I’m going to gain weight, I have to undo this.” And then I end up in the bathroom making myself throw up.\n\nI’m here because it’s gotten to where after I eat too much, I keep doing that — I make myself throw up so I won’t gain weight — and it scares me because once that thought starts, it feels like I can’t stop myself. I know it probably sounds repetitive, but that’s exactly what it feels like in the moment: like if I don’t get it out, I’ve lost control of my weight completely.\n\nDoctor: Are there times when you eat a large amount of food in a short period, but you don't make yourself throw up afterward?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, yes… but it’s not common. I mean, there are times when I eat a lot quickly and I don’t throw up afterward, but even then it’s like my head doesn’t let it go. I’ll sit there feeling panicked and guilty, thinking about what I ate and whether it’s going to make me gain weight.\n\nMost of the time, though, I do end up going to the bathroom and making myself throw up. That’s really why I’m here — because after I eat too much, I keep doing it so I won’t gain weight, and it’s gotten to the point where I’m scared I can’t stop myself. Even if I tell myself I’m not going to, once I feel like I’ve eaten too much, it starts feeling almost automatic.\n\nDoctor: Do you make yourself throw up after almost every episode of eating a large amount of food in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: Yes… almost every time. If I eat what feels like too much, especially if it happens fast, I pretty much go straight into that panic in my head. I start thinking, “I can’t let this stay in me, I’m going to gain weight,” and then I end up in the bathroom making myself throw up.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound like it’s some casual thing, because it doesn’t feel casual at all. It feels like once I’ve crossed that line with food, I don’t know how to just sit with it. I tell myself I should stop, or that I shouldn’t do it this time, but then the fear gets so loud that I feel like I have to. That’s really why I’m here — after I eat too much, I keep making myself throw up so I won’t gain weight, and it’s gotten to where I’m scared I can’t stop doing it.\n\nDoctor: Is making yourself throw up always about trying to control your weight?\n\nPatient: I mean… I think most of the time it is, yes. That’s the part I’m embarrassed to say out loud, but it’s true. After I eat too much, it’s like my brain goes straight to, “You’re going to gain weight, you have to fix this.” And then I end up going to the bathroom and making myself throw up.\n\nIt’s not like I sit there calmly deciding it. It feels more frantic than that. Sometimes I tell myself, “Don’t do it this time, just leave it alone,” but the fear gets so strong that I feel like I can’t stand it. So yes, for me it’s tied to trying not to gain weight, and it’s gotten to where I’m scared because I keep doing it and I don’t feel like I can stop once that panic starts.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel relieved or less anxious after making yourself throw up?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do, at least at first. It’s like the panic spikes so high after I eat too much that making myself throw up feels like the only way to get it to come down. I hate saying that, because I know it sounds terrible, but in that moment I’m thinking, “Okay, now maybe I won’t gain weight from this,” and there’s this immediate kind of relief.\n\nBut it doesn’t last very long. Afterward I usually feel ashamed and scared, because I’m standing there in the bathroom realizing I did it again, even after I told myself I wouldn’t. That’s really what scares me — it’s gotten to where after I eat too much, I keep making myself throw up so I won’t gain weight, and I don’t feel like I can stop once that fear takes over.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes… that’s probably the part that scares me the most, honestly. It doesn’t feel like normal eating where I’m choosing what I want and then I stop when I’ve had enough. Once I start on those nights, it’s like my hands just keep reaching for more food even while my brain is saying, “Lucy, knock it off, stop, what are you doing?” And I still don’t stop.

It’s almost like I’m watching myself do it, if that makes sense. I’ll be telling myself I’ve already eaten too much, I need to put it away, I need to walk out of the kitchen, but then I’m still opening things, still grabbing bites, still going back for more. I don’t really feel back in charge until it’s already over, and then I’m left sitting there realizing how much I ate. So yes, during it, it really does feel like I don’t have control.

Even if I tell myself I’m not going to, once I feel like I’ve eaten too much, it starts feeling almost automatic.\n\nDoctor: Do you make yourself throw up after almost every episode of eating a large amount of food in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: Yes… almost every time. If I eat what feels like too much, especially if it happens fast, I pretty much go straight into that panic in my head. I start thinking, “I can’t let this stay in me, I’m going to gain weight,” and then I end up in the bathroom making myself throw up.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound like it’s some casual thing, because it doesn’t feel casual at all. It feels like once I’ve crossed that line with food, I don’t know how to just sit with it. I tell myself I should stop, or that I shouldn’t do it this time, but then the fear gets so loud that I feel like I have to. That’s really why I’m here — after I eat too much, I keep making myself throw up so I won’t gain weight, and it’s gotten to where I’m scared I can’t stop doing it.\n\nDoctor: Is making yourself throw up always about trying to control your weight?\n\nPatient: I mean… I think most of the time it is, yes. That’s the part I’m embarrassed to say out loud, but it’s true. After I eat too much, it’s like my brain goes straight to, “You’re going to gain weight, you have to fix this.” And then I end up going to the bathroom and making myself throw up.\n\nIt’s not like I sit there calmly deciding it. It feels more frantic than that. Sometimes I tell myself, “Don’t do it this time, just leave it alone,” but the fear gets so strong that I feel like I can’t stand it. So yes, for me it’s tied to trying not to gain weight, and it’s gotten to where I’m scared because I keep doing it and I don’t feel like I can stop once that panic starts.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel relieved or less anxious after making yourself throw up?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do, at least at first. It’s like the panic spikes so high after I eat too much that making myself throw up feels like the only way to get it to come down. I hate saying that, because I know it sounds terrible, but in that moment I’m thinking, “Okay, now maybe I won’t gain weight from this,” and there’s this immediate kind of relief.\n\nBut it doesn’t last very long. Afterward I usually feel ashamed and scared, because I’m standing there in the bathroom realizing I did it again, even after I told myself I wouldn’t. That’s really what scares me — it’s gotten to where after I eat too much, I keep making myself throw up so I won’t gain weight, and I don’t feel like I can stop once that fear takes over.\n\nDoctor: Do you typically feel a lack of control during these episodes of eating a large amount of food in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: Yes… that’s probably the part that scares me the most, honestly. It doesn’t feel like normal eating where I’m choosing what I want and then I stop when I’ve had enough. Once I start on those nights, it’s like my hands just keep reaching for more food even while my brain is saying, “Lucy, knock it off, stop, what are you doing?” And I still don’t stop.\n\nIt’s almost like I’m watching myself do it, if that makes sense. I’ll be telling myself I’ve already eaten too much, I need to put it away, I need to walk out of the kitchen, but then I’m still opening things, still grabbing bites, still going back for more. I don’t really feel back in charge until it’s already over, and then I’m left sitting there realizing how much I ate. So yes, during it, it really does feel like I don’t have control.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. That’s really what scares me — it’s gotten to where after I eat too much, I keep making myself throw up so I won’t gain weight, and I don’t feel like I can stop once that fear takes over.\n\nDoctor: Do you typically feel a lack of control during these episodes of eating a large amount of food in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: Yes… that’s probably the part that scares me the most, honestly. It doesn’t feel like normal eating where I’m choosing what I want and then I stop when I’ve had enough. Once I start on those nights, it’s like my hands just keep reaching for more food even while my brain is saying, “Lucy, knock it off, stop, what are you doing?” And I still don’t stop.\n\nIt’s almost like I’m watching myself do it, if that makes sense. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes… definitely at least twice a week. I hate how clear the answer is, but yeah.

For the past three months or so, I honestly don’t think there’s been many weeks where it didn’t happen at all. Most weeks there’s at least one night where I eat way too much in a short time, usually late at night, and then I end up going and making myself throw up afterward. And some weeks it’s more than that — several times — especially when I’m home more because my back is bothering me and I’m just around the house more.

It’s not like I can say, “Oh, that was just one bad week.” It’s been pretty consistent, and that’s part of why I’m scared to talk about it, because when I say it out loud it sounds worse than how I try to explain it to myself in my head.
